 \"length\", PropertyDescriptor { `[[Value]]`:
 [𝔽](notational-conventions.html#%F0%9D%94%BD)(`length`),
 `[[Writable]]`: false, `[[Enumerable]]`: false, `[[Configurable]]`: true }).
3. Return unused.

# [10.2.11] FunctionDeclarationInstantiation ( `func`, `argList` )

The abstract operation FunctionDeclarationInstantiation takes arguments
`func` (an ECMAScript [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)) and
`argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
unused or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
`func` is the [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) for which
the [execution
context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-execution-contexts)
is being established.

[Note]

::: note-contents
When an [execution
context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-execution-contexts)
is established for evaluating an ECMAScript
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) a new
[Function Environment
Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-function-environment-records)
is created and bindings for each formal parameter are instantiated in
that [Environment
Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records).
Each declaration in the
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) body
is also instantiated. If the
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)\'s
formal parameters do not include any default value initializers then the
body declarations are instantiated in the same [Environment
Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
as the parameters. If default value parameter initializers exist, a
second [Environment
Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
is created for the body declarations. Formal parameters and
[functions](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) are
initialized as part of FunctionDeclarationInstantiation. All other
bindings are initialized during evaluation of the
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) body.

It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `calleeContext` be the [running execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
2. Let `code` be
 `func`.`[[ECMAScriptCode]]`.
3. Let `strict` be `func`.`[[Strict]]`.
4. Let `formals` be
 `func`.`[[FormalParameters]]`.
5. Let `paramNames` be the
 [BoundNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-boundnames)
 of `formals`.
6. If `paramNames` has any duplicate entries, let
 `hasDuplicates` be true; else let
 `hasDuplicates` be false.
7. Let `simpleParamList` be
 [IsSimpleParameterList](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-static-semantics-issimpleparameterlist)
 of `formals`.
8. Let `hasParamExprs` be
 [ContainsExpression](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-static-semantics-containsexpression)
 of `formals`.
9. Let `variableNames` be the
 [VarDeclaredNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-vardeclarednames)
 of `code`.
10. Let `variableDecls` be the
 [VarScopedDeclarations](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-varscopeddeclarations)
 of `code`.
11. Let `lexicalNames` be the
 [LexicallyDeclaredNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-lexicallydeclarednames)
 of `code`.
12. Let `funcNames` be a new empty
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type).
13. Let `funcsToInitialize` be a new empty
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type).
14. For each element `variableDecl` of
 `variableDecls`, in reverse
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
 order, do
 1. If `variableDecl` is neither a
 [VariableDeclaration](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-VariableDeclaration)
 nor a
 [ForBinding](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-ForBinding)
 nor a
 [BindingIdentifier](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#prod-BindingIdentifier),
 then
 1. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `variableDecl` is either a
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration),
 a
 [GeneratorDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-GeneratorDeclaration),
 an
 [AsyncFunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-AsyncFunctionDeclaration),
 or an
 [AsyncGeneratorDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-AsyncGeneratorDeclaration).
 2. Let `funcName` be the sole element of the
 [BoundNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-boundnames)
 of `variableDecl`.
 3. If `funcNames` does not contain
 `funcName`, then
 1. Insert `funcName` as the first element of
 `funcNames`.
 2. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): If there
 are multiple
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
 declarations for the same name, the last declaration is
 used.
 3. Insert `variableDecl` as the first element of
 `funcsToInitialize`.
15. Let `argumentsObjNeeded` be true.
16. If `func`.`[[ThisMode]]` is lexical,
 then
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): Arrow
 [functions](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
 never have an arguments object.
 2. Set `argumentsObjNeeded` to false.
17. Else if `paramNames` contains \"arguments\", then
 1. Set `argumentsObjNeeded` to false.
18. Else if `hasParamExprs` is false, then
 1. If `funcNames` contains \"arguments\" or
 `lexicalNames` contains \"arguments\", then
 1. Set `argumentsObjNeeded` to false.
19. If `strict` is true or `hasParamExprs` is
 false, then
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): Only a single
 [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
 is needed for the parameters, since calls to `eval` in [strict
 mode
 code](ecmascript-language-source-code.html#sec-strict-mode-code)
 cannot create new bindings which are visible outside of the
 `eval`.
 2. Let `envRecord` be the LexicalEnvironment of
 `calleeContext`.
20. Else,
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A separate
 [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
 is needed to ensure that bindings created by [direct
 eval](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#sec-function-calls-runtime-semantics-evaluation)
 calls in the formal parameter list are outside the environment
 where parameters are declared.
 2. Let `calleeEnv` be the LexicalEnvironment of
 `calleeContext`.
 3. Let `envRecord` be
 [NewDeclarativeEnvironment](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-newdeclarativeenvironment)(`calleeEnv`).
 4. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): The
 VariableEnvironment of `calleeContext` and
 `calleeEnv` are the same [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records).
 5. Set the LexicalEnvironment of `calleeContext` to
 `envRecord`.
21. For each String `paramName` of `paramNames`,
 do
 1. Let `alreadyDeclared` be
 ! `envRecord`.[HasBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-hasbinding)(`paramName`).
 2. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): [Early
 errors](error-handling-and-language-extensions.html#early-error)
 ensure that duplicate parameter names can only occur in
 [non-strict
 functions](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#non-strict-function)
 that do not have parameter default values or rest parameters.
 3. If `alreadyDeclared` is false, then
 1. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(`paramName`,
 false).
 2. If `hasDuplicates` is true, then
 1. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[InitializeBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-initializebinding)(`paramName`,
 undefined).
22. If `argumentsObjNeeded` is true, then
 1. If `strict` is true or `simpleParamList`
 is false, then
 1. Let `argumentsObj` be
 [CreateUnmappedArgumentsObject](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-createunmappedargumentsobject)(`argList`).
 2. Else,
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A mapped
 argument object is only provided for [non-strict
 functions](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#non-strict-function)
 that don\'t have a rest parameter, any parameter default
 value initializers, or any destructured parameters.
 2. Let `argumentsObj` be
 [CreateMappedArgumentsObject](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-createmappedargumentsobject)(`func`,
 `formals`, `argList`,
 `envRecord`).
 3. If `strict` is true, then
 1. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[CreateImmutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createimmutablebinding)(\"arguments\",
 false).
 2. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): In [strict
 mode
 code](ecmascript-language-source-code.html#sec-strict-mode-code)
 [early
 errors](error-handling-and-language-extensions.html#early-error)
 prevent attempting to assign to this binding, so its
 mutability is not observable.
 4. Else,
 1. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(\"arguments\",
 false).
 5. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[InitializeBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-initializebinding)(\"arguments\",
 `argumentsObj`).
 6. Let `paramBindings` be the
 [list-concatenation](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#list-concatenation)
 of `paramNames` and « \"arguments\" ».
23. Else,
 1. Let `paramBindings` be `paramNames`.
24. Let `iteratorRecord` be
 [CreateListIteratorRecord](abstract-operations.html#sec-createlistiteratorRecord)(`argList`).
25. If `hasDuplicates` is true, then
 1. Let `usedEnv` be undefined.
26. Else,
 1. Let `usedEnv` be `envRecord`.
27. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): The following step
 cannot return a
 [ReturnCompletion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-returncompletion)
 because the only way such a completion can arise in expression
 position is by use of
 [YieldExpression](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-YieldExpression),
 which is forbidden in parameter lists by Early Error rules in
 [15.5.1](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-generator-function-definitions-static-semantics-early-errors)
 and
 [15.6.1](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-async-generator-function-definitions-static-semantics-early-errors).
28. Perform
 ? [IteratorBindingInitialization](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-runtime-semantics-iteratorbindinginitialization)
 of `formals` with arguments `iteratorRecord`
 and `usedEnv`.
29. If `hasParamExprs` is false, then
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): Only a single
 [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
 is needed for the parameters and top-level vars.
 2. Let `instantiatedVariableNames` be a copy of the
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
 `paramBindings`.
 3. For each element `name` of
 `variableNames`, do
 1. If `instantiatedVariableNames` does not contain
 `name`, then
 1. Append `name` to
 `instantiatedVariableNames`.
 2. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(`name`,
 false).
 3. Perform
 ! `envRecord`.[InitializeBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-initializebinding)(`name`,
 undefined).
 4. Let `variableEnv` be `envRecord`.
30. Else,
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A separate
 [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
 is needed to ensure that closures created by expressions in the
 formal parameter list do not have visibility of declarations in
 the
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
 body.
 2. Let `variableEnv` be
 [NewDeclarativeEnvironment](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-newdeclarativeenvironment)(`envRecord`).
 3. Set the VariableEnvironment of `calleeContext` to
 `variableEnv`.
 4. Let `instantiatedVariableNames` be a new empty
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type).
 5. For each element `name` of
 `variableNames`, do
 1. If `instantiatedVariableNames` does not contain
 `name`, then
 1. Append `name` to
 `instantiatedVariableNames`.
 2. Perform
 ! `variableEnv`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(`name`,
 false).
 3. If `paramBindings` does not contain
 `name` or `funcNames` contains
 `name`, then
 1. Let `initialValue` be undefined.
 4. Else,
 1. Let `initialValue` be
 ! `envRecord`.[GetBindingValue](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-getbindingvalue)(`name`,
 false).
 5. Perform
 ! `variableEnv`.[InitializeBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-initializebinding)(`name`,
 `initialValue`).
 6. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A var
 with the same name as a formal parameter initially has
 the same value as the corresponding initialized
 parameter.
31. If `strict` is true, then
 1. Let `lexicalEnv` be `variableEnv`.
32. Else,
 1. ::::
 ::: attributes-tag
 [Normative Optional](conformance.html#sec-conformance)
 :::

 If the [host](overview.html#host) is a web browser or otherwise
 supports [Block-Level Function Declarations Web Legacy
 Compatibility
 Semantics](additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers.html#sec-block-level-function-declarations-web-legacy-compatibility-semantics),
 then
 1. For each
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration)
 `funcDecl` that is directly contained in the
 [StatementList](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-StatementList)
 of any
 [Block](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-Block),
 [CaseClause](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-CaseClause),
 or
 [DefaultClause](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-DefaultClause)
 `x` such that `code`
 [Contains](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-contains)
 `x` is true, do
 1. Let `funcName` be the
 [StringValue](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#sec-static-semantics-stringvalue)
 of the
 [BindingIdentifier](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#prod-BindingIdentifier)
 of `funcDecl`.
 2. If replacing the
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration)
 `funcDecl` with a
 [VariableStatement](ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#prod-VariableStatement)
 that has `funcName` as a
 [BindingIdentifier](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#prod-BindingIdentifier)
 would not produce any Early Errors for `func`
 and `paramNames` does not contain
 `funcName`, then
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A var
 binding for `funcName` is only
 instantiated here if it is neither a
 VarDeclaredName, the name of a formal parameter, or
 another
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration).
 2. If `instantiatedVariableNames` does not
 contain `funcName` and
 `funcName` is not \"arguments\", then
 1. Perform
 ! `variableEnv`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(`funcName`,
 false).
 2. Perform
 ! `variableEnv`.[InitializeBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-initializebinding)(`funcName`,
 undefined).
 3. Append `funcName` to
 `instantiatedVariableNames`.
 3. [When the
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration)
 `funcDecl` is evaluated, perform the
 following steps in place of the
 [FunctionDeclaration](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#prod-FunctionDeclaration)
 [Evaluation](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-evaluation)
 algorithm provided in
 [15.2.6](ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-function-definitions-runtime-semantics-evaluation):]
 1. Let `funcEnv` be the [running
 execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context)\'s
 VariableEnvironment.
 2. Let `blockEnv` be the [running
 execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context)\'s
 LexicalEnvironment.
 3. Let `funcObj` be
 ! `blockEnv`.[GetBindingValue](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-getbindingvalue)(`funcName`,
 false).
 4. Perform
 ! `funcEnv`.[SetMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-setmutablebinding)(`funcName`,
 `funcObj`, false).
 5. Return unused.
 ::::

 2. Let `lexicalEnv` be
 [NewDeclarativeEnvironment](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-newdeclarativeenvironment)(`variableEnv`).

 3. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): [Non-strict
 functions](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#non-strict-function)
 use a separate [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records)
 for top-level lexical declarations so that a [direct
 eval](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#sec-function-calls-runtime-semantics-evaluation)
 can determine whether any var scoped declarations introduced by
 the eval code conflict with pre-existing top-level lexically
 scoped declarations. This is not needed for [strict
 functions](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#strict-function)
 because a strict [direct
 eval](ecmascript-language-expressions.html#sec-function-calls-runtime-semantics-evaluation)
 always places all declarations into a new [Environment
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-environment-records).
33. Set the LexicalEnvironment of `calleeContext` to
 `lexicalEnv`.
34. Let `lexicalDecls` be the
 [LexicallyScopedDeclarations](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-lexicallyscopeddeclarations)
 of `code`.
35. For each element `lexicalDecl` of
 `lexicalDecls`, do
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): A lexically
 declared name cannot be the same as a
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)/generator
 declaration, formal parameter, or a var name. Lexically declared
 names are only instantiated here but not initialized.
 2. For each element `name` of the
 [BoundNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-boundnames)
 of `lexicalDecl`, do
 1. If
 [IsConstantDeclaration](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-isconstantdeclaration)
 of `lexicalDecl` is true, then
 1. Perform
 ! `lexicalEnv`.[CreateImmutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createimmutablebinding)(`name`,
 true).
 2. Else,
 1. Perform
 ! `lexicalEnv`.[CreateMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-createmutablebinding)(`name`,
 false).
36. Let `privateEnv` be the PrivateEnvironment of
 `calleeContext`.
37. For each [Parse Node](notational-conventions.html#sec-parsing)
 `funcDecl` of `funcsToInitialize`, do
 1. Let `funcName` be the sole element of the
 [BoundNames](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-static-semantics-boundnames)
 of `funcDecl`.
 2. Let `funcObj` be
 [InstantiateFunctionDeclaration](syntax-directed-operations.html#sec-runtime-semantics-instantiatefunctiondeclaration)
 of `funcDecl` with arguments `lexicalEnv`
 and `privateEnv`.
 3. Perform
 ! `variableEnv`.[SetMutableBinding](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#abstract-setmutablebinding)(`funcName`,
 `funcObj`, false).
38. Return unused.

# [10.3] Built-in Function Objects

A [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object is an [ordinary
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object); it must
satisfy the requirements for [ordinary
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object) set out
in
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots).

In addition to the internal slots required of every [ordinary
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object) (see
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots)),
a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object must also have the following internal slots:

- `[[Realm]]`, a [Realm
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm-record) that
 represents the
 [realm](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm) in which
 the [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
 was created.
- `[[InitialName]]`, a String that is the initial name
 of the
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object). It
 is used by
 [20.2.3.5](fundamental-objects.html#sec-function.prototype.tostring).
- `[[Async]]`, a Boolean that indicates whether the
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) has
 async
 [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) call
 and construct behaviour in
 [BuiltinCallOrConstruct](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-builtincallorconstruct).

The initial value of a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object\'s `[[Prototype]]` internal slot is
[%Function.prototype%](fundamental-objects.html#sec-properties-of-the-function-prototype-object),
unless otherwise specified.

A [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object must have a `[[Call]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) that conforms to
the definition in
[10.3.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-built-in-function-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist).

A [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object has a `[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) if and only if it
is described as a
"[constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)", or
some algorithm in this specification explicitly sets its
`[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method). Such a
`[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) must conform to
the definition in
[10.3.2](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-built-in-function-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget).

An implementation may provide additional [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
objects that are not defined in this specification.

# [10.3.1] `[[Call]]` ( `thisArg`, `argList` )

The `[[Call]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object `func` takes arguments `thisArg` (an
[ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and `argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)
or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Return
 ? [BuiltinCallOrConstruct](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-builtincallorconstruct)(`func`,
 `thisArg`, `argList`, undefined).

# [10.3.2] `[[Construct]]` ( `argList`, `newTarget` )

The `[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object `func` (when the
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) is present) takes
arguments `argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and `newTarget` (a
[constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)) and
returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an Object or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `result` be
 ? [BuiltinCallOrConstruct](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-builtincallorconstruct)(`func`,
 uninitialized, `argList`, `newTarget`).
2. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): `result`
 [is an
 Object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type).
3. Return `result`.

# [10.3.3] BuiltinCallOrConstruct ( `func`, `thisArg`, `argList`, `newTarget` )

The abstract operation BuiltinCallOrConstruct takes arguments
`func` (a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object), `thisArg` (an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)
or uninitialized), `argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)),
and `newTarget` (a
[constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor) or
undefined) and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)
or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `callerContext` be the [running execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
2. If `callerContext` is not already suspended, suspend
 `callerContext`.
3. Let `calleeContext` be a new [execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-execution-contexts).
4. Set the
 [Function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) of
 `calleeContext` to `func`.
5. Let `calleeRealm` be
 `func`.`[[Realm]]`.
6. Set the [Realm](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm)
 of `calleeContext` to `calleeRealm`.
7. Set the ScriptOrModule of `calleeContext` to null.
8. Perform any necessary
 [implementation-defined](overview.html#implementation-defined)
 initialization of `calleeContext`.
9. Push `calleeContext` onto the [execution context
 stack](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#execution-context-stack);
 `calleeContext` is now the [running execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
10. If `func`.`[[Async]]` is true, then
 1. Let `promiseCapability` be
 ! [NewPromiseCapability](control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-newpromisecapability)([%Promise%](control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-promise-constructor)).
 2. Let `resultsClosure` be a new [Abstract
 Closure](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-abstract-closure)
 with no parameters that captures `func`,
 `thisArg`, `argList`, and
 `newTarget` and performs the following steps when
 called:
 1. Let `result` be the [Completion
 Record](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
 that is [the result of evaluating]
 `func` in a manner that conforms to the
 specification of `func`. If `thisArg`
 is uninitialized, the this value is uninitialized; else
 `thisArg` provides the this value.
 `argList` provides the named parameters.
 `newTarget` provides the NewTarget value.
 2. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): If
 `func` is defined in this document, "the
 specification of `func`" is the behaviour
 specified for it via algorithm steps or other means.
 3. Return
 [Completion](notational-conventions.html#sec-completion-ao)(`result`).
 3. Perform
 [AsyncFunctionStart](control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-async-functions-abstract-operations-async-function-start)(`promiseCapability`,
 `resultsClosure`).
 4. Remove `calleeContext` from the [execution context
 stack](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#execution-context-stack)
 and restore `callerContext` as the [running execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
 5. Return `promiseCapability`.`[[Promise]]`.
11. [Let `result` be the [Completion
 Record](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
 that is [the result of evaluating] `func`
 in a manner that conforms to the specification of `func`.
 If `thisArg` is uninitialized, the this value is
 uninitialized; else `thisArg` provides the this value.
 `argList` provides the named parameters.
 `newTarget` provides the NewTarget
 value.]
12. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): If `func`
 is defined in this document, "the specification of
 `func`" is the behaviour specified for it via algorithm
 steps or other means.
13. Remove `calleeContext` from the [execution context
 stack](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#execution-context-stack)
 and restore `callerContext` as the [running execution
 context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
14. Return ? `result`.

[Note]

::: note-contents
When `calleeContext` is removed from the [execution context
stack](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#execution-context-stack)
it must not be destroyed if it has been suspended and retained by an
accessible Generator for later resumption.

# [10.3.4] CreateBuiltinFunction ( `behaviour`, `length`, `name`, `additionalInternalSlotsList` \[ , `realm` \[ , `proto` \[ , `prefix` \[ , `async` \] \] \] \] )

The abstract operation CreateBuiltinFunction takes arguments
`behaviour` (an [Abstract
Closure](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-abstract-closure), a
set of algorithm steps, or some other definition of a
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)\'s
behaviour provided in this specification), `length` (a
non-negative [integer](notational-conventions.html#integer) or +∞),
`name` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key) or a [Private
Name](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-private-names)), and
`additionalInternalSlotsList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of names of internal slots) and optional arguments `realm` (a
[Realm
Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm-record)),
`proto` (an Object or null), `prefix` (a String),
and `async` (a Boolean) and returns a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object. `additionalInternalSlotsList` contains the names of
additional internal slots that must be defined as part of the object.
This operation creates a [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
object. It performs the following steps when called:

1. If `realm` is not present, set `realm` to [the
 current Realm
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#current-realm).
2. If `proto` is not present, set `proto` to
 `realm`.`[[Intrinsics]]`.\[\[[%Function.prototype%](fundamental-objects.html#sec-properties-of-the-function-prototype-object)\]\].
3. If `async` is not present, set `async` to
 false.
4. Let `internalSlotsList` be a
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
 containing the names of all the internal slots that
 [10.3](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-built-in-function-objects)
 requires for the [built-in
 function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
 object that is about to be created.
5. Append the elements of `additionalInternalSlotsList` to
 `internalSlotsList`.
6. Let `func` be a new [built-in
 function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
 object that, when called, performs the action described by
 `behaviour` using the provided arguments as the values of
 the corresponding parameters specified by `behaviour`.
 The new [function
 object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) has
 internal slots whose names are the elements of
 `internalSlotsList`, and an `[[InitialName]]` internal slot.
7. Set `func`.`[[Async]]` to
 `async`.
8. Set `func`.`[[Prototype]]` to
 `proto`.
9. Set `func`.`[[Extensible]]` to true.
10. Set `func`.`[[Realm]]` to
 `realm`.
11. Set `func`.`[[InitialName]]` to null.
12. Perform
 [SetFunctionLength](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-setfunctionlength)(`func`,
 `length`).
13. If `prefix` is not present, then
 1. Perform
 [SetFunctionName](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-setfunctionname)(`func`,
 `name`).
14. Else,
 1. Perform
 [SetFunctionName](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-setfunctionname)(`func`,
 `name`, `prefix`).
15. Return `func`.

Each [built-in
function](ecmascript-standard-built-in-objects.html#built-in-function)
defined in this specification is created by calling the
CreateBuiltinFunction abstract operation.

# [10.4] Built-in Exotic Object Internal Methods and Slots

This specification defines several kinds of built-in [exotic
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object). These
objects generally behave similar to [ordinary
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object) except
for a few specific situations. The following [exotic
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object) use the
[ordinary object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object)
internal [methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) except
where it is explicitly specified otherwise below:

# [10.4.1] Bound Function Exotic Objects

A [bound function exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
is an [exotic
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object) that wraps
another [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object). A [bound
function exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
is callable (it has a `[[Call]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) and may have a
`[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method)). Calling a
[bound function exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
generally results in a call of its wrapped
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object).

An object is a [bound function exotic
object] if its `[[Call]]` and (if applicable) `[[Construct]]` internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
following implementations, and its other essential internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
definitions found in
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots).
These [methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) are
installed in
[BoundFunctionCreate](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-boundfunctioncreate).

[Bound function exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
do not have the internal slots of ECMAScript [function
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) listed
in [Table
26](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#table-internal-slots-of-ecmascript-function-objects).
Instead they have the internal slots listed in [Table
27](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#table-internal-slots-of-bound-function-exotic-objects),
in addition to `[[Prototype]]` and
`[[Extensible]]`.

<figure>
<span id="table-28"></span>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Internal Slot</th>
<th>Type</th>
<th>Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><var class="field">[[BoundTargetFunction]]</var></td>
<td>a callable Object</td>
<td>The wrapped <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object">function
object</a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><var class="field">[[BoundThis]]</var></td>
<td>an <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript
language value</a></td>
<td>The value that is always passed as the this value when calling the
wrapped <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object">function</a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><var class="field">[[BoundArguments]]</var></td>
<td>a <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a>
of <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript
language values</a></td>
<td>A list of values whose elements are used as the first arguments to
any call to the wrapped <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object">function</a>.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption>Table 27: Internal Slots of Bound <a
href="ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object">Function</a>
Exotic Objects</figcaption>
</figure>

# [10.4.1.1] `[[Call]]` ( `thisArg`, `argList` )

The `[[Call]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [bound
function exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
`func` takes arguments `thisArg` (an [ECMAScript
language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and `argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)
or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `target` be
 `func`.`[[BoundTargetFunction]]`.
2. Let `boundThis` be
 `func`.`[[BoundThis]]`.
3. Let `boundArgs` be
 `func`.`[[BoundArguments]]`.
4. Let `args` be the
 [list-concatenation](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#list-concatenation)
 of `boundArgs` and `argList`.
5. Return
 ? [Call](abstract-operations.html#sec-call)(`target`,
 `boundThis`, `args`).

# [10.4.1.2] `[[Construct]]` ( `argList`, `newTarget` )

The `[[Construct]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [bound
function exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object)
`func` takes arguments `argList` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and `newTarget` (a
[constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)) and
returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an Object or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `target` be
 `func`.`[[BoundTargetFunction]]`.
2. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 [IsConstructor](abstract-operations.html#sec-isconstructor)(`target`)
 is true.
3. Let `boundArgs` be
 `func`.`[[BoundArguments]]`.
4. Let `args` be the
 [list-concatenation](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#list-concatenation)
 of `boundArgs` and `argList`.
5. If
 [SameValue](abstract-operations.html#sec-samevalue)(`func`,
 `newTarget`) is true, set `newTarget` to
 `target`.
6. Return
 ? [Construct](abstract-operations.html#sec-construct)(`target`,
 `args`, `newTarget`).

# [10.4.1.3] BoundFunctionCreate ( `targetFunc`, `boundThis`, `boundArgs` )

The abstract operation BoundFunctionCreate takes arguments
`targetFunc` (a [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)),
`boundThis` (an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)),
and `boundArgs` (a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [ECMAScript language
values](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) or a
[throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It is used to specify the creation of new [bound function exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#bound-function-exotic-object).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `proto` be
 ? [`targetFunc`.[\[\[GetPrototypeOf\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-getprototypeof)()].
2. Let `internalSlotsList` be the
 [list-concatenation](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#list-concatenation)
 of « `[[Prototype]]`, `[[Extensible]]` » and the internal slots listed in [Table
 27](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#table-internal-slots-of-bound-function-exotic-objects).
3. Let `obj` be
 [MakeBasicObject](abstract-operations.html#sec-makebasicobject)(`internalSlotsList`).
4. Set `obj`.`[[Prototype]]` to
 `proto`.
5. Set
 `obj`.[\[\[Call\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-call)
 as specified in
 [10.4.1.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-bound-function-exotic-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist).
6. If
 [IsConstructor](abstract-operations.html#sec-isconstructor)(`targetFunc`)
 is true, then
 1. Set
 `obj`.[\[\[Construct\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-construct)
 as specified in
 [10.4.1.2](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-bound-function-exotic-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget).
7. Set `obj`.`[[BoundTargetFunction]]` to
 `targetFunc`.
8. Set `obj`.`[[BoundThis]]` to
 `boundThis`.
9. Set `obj`.`[[BoundArguments]]` to
 `boundArgs`.
10. Return `obj`.

# [10.4.2] Array Exotic Objects

An Array is an [exotic
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object) that gives
special treatment to [array
index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index) [property
keys](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key) (see
[6.1.7](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type)). A
property whose [property
name](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-name) is an [array
index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index) is also called
an *element*. Every Array has a non-configurable \"length\" property
whose value is always a non-negative [integral
Number](notational-conventions.html#integral-number) whose [mathematical
value](notational-conventions.html#mathematical-value) is strictly less
than 2^32^. The value of the \"length\" property is numerically greater
than the name of every [own
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) whose name
is an [array index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index);
whenever an [own
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) of an
Array is created or changed, other properties are adjusted as necessary
to maintain this invariant. Specifically, whenever an [own
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) is added
whose name is an [array
index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index), the value of
the \"length\" property is changed, if necessary, to be one more than
the numeric value of that [array
index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index); and whenever
the value of the \"length\" property is changed, every [own
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) whose name
is an [array index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index)
whose value is not smaller than the new length is deleted. This
constraint applies only to [own
properties](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) of an
Array and is unaffected by \"length\" or [array
index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index) properties
that may be inherited from its
[prototypes](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#prototype).

An object is an [Array exotic object] (or simply, an Array) if
its `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) uses the
following implementation, and its other essential internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
definitions found in
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots).
These [methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) are
installed in
[ArrayCreate](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-arraycreate).

# [10.4.2.1] `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` ( `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc` )

The `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [Array
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#array-exotic-object)
`array` takes arguments `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and
`propertyDesc` (a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. If `propertyKey` is \"length\", return
 ? [ArraySetLength](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-arraysetlength)(`array`,
 `propertyDesc`).
2. If `propertyKey` is an [array
 index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index), then
 1. Let `lengthDesc` be
 [OrdinaryGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarygetownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\").
 2. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `lengthDesc` is not undefined.
 3. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 [IsDataDescriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-isdatadescriptor)(`lengthDesc`)
 is true.
 4. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `lengthDesc`.`[[Configurable]]` is
 false.
 5. Let `length` be
 `lengthDesc`.`[[Value]]`.
 6. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `length` is a non-negative [integral
 Number](notational-conventions.html#integral-number).
 7. Let `index` be
 ! [ToUint32](abstract-operations.html#sec-touint32)(`propertyKey`).
 8. If `index` ≥ `length` and
 `lengthDesc`.`[[Writable]]` is
 false, return false.
 9. Let `succeeded` be
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc`).
 10. If `succeeded` is false, return false.
 11. If `index` ≥ `length`, then
 1. Set `lengthDesc`.`[[Value]]` to
 `index` + 1~𝔽~.
 2. Set `succeeded` to
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", `lengthDesc`).
 3. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `succeeded` is true.
 12. Return true.
3. Return
 ? [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc`).

# [10.4.2.2] ArrayCreate ( `length` \[ , `proto` \] )

The abstract operation ArrayCreate takes argument `length` (a
non-negative [integer](notational-conventions.html#integer)) and
optional argument `proto` (an Object) and returns either a
[normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an [Array exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#array-exotic-object)
or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It is used to specify the creation of new Arrays. It performs the
following steps when called:

1. If `length` \> 2^32^ - 1, throw a RangeError exception.
2. If `proto` is not present, set `proto` to
 [%Array.prototype%](indexed-collections.html#sec-properties-of-the-array-prototype-object).
3. Let `array` be
 [MakeBasicObject](abstract-operations.html#sec-makebasicobject)(«
 `[[Prototype]]`, `[[Extensible]]` »).
4. Set `array`.`[[Prototype]]` to
 `proto`.
5. Set
 `array`.[\[\[DefineOwnProperty\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-defineownproperty)
 as specified in
 [10.4.2.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-array-exotic-objects-defineownproperty-p-desc).
6. Perform
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", PropertyDescriptor { `[[Value]]`:
 [𝔽](notational-conventions.html#%F0%9D%94%BD)(`length`),
 `[[Writable]]`: true, `[[Enumerable]]`: false, `[[Configurable]]`: false }).
7. Return `array`.

# [10.4.2.3] ArraySpeciesCreate ( `originalArray`, `length` )

The abstract operation ArraySpeciesCreate takes arguments
`originalArray` (an Object) and `length` (a
non-negative [integer](notational-conventions.html#integer)) and returns
either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an Object or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It is used to specify the creation of a new Array or similar object
using a [constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) that
is derived from `originalArray`. It does not enforce that the
[constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
returns an Array. It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `isArray` be
 ? [IsArray](abstract-operations.html#sec-isarray)(`originalArray`).
2. If `isArray` is false, return
 ? [ArrayCreate](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-arraycreate)(`length`).
3. Let `ctor` be
 ? [Get](abstract-operations.html#sec-get-o-p)(`originalArray`,
 \"constructor\").
4. If
 [IsConstructor](abstract-operations.html#sec-isconstructor)(`ctor`)
 is true, then
 1. Let `thisRealm` be [the current Realm
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#current-realm).
 2. Let `ctorRealm` be
 ? [GetFunctionRealm](abstract-operations.html#sec-getfunctionrealm)(`ctor`).
 3. If `thisRealm` and `ctorRealm` are not the
 same [Realm
 Record](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm-record),
 then
 1. If
 [SameValue](abstract-operations.html#sec-samevalue)(`ctor`,
 `ctorRealm`.`[[Intrinsics]]`.\[\[[%Array%](indexed-collections.html#sec-array-constructor)\]\])
 is true, set `ctor` to undefined.
5. If `ctor` [is an
 Object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type), then
 1. Set `ctor` to
 ? [Get](abstract-operations.html#sec-get-o-p)(`ctor`,
 [%Symbol.species%](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-well-known-symbols)).
 2. If `ctor` is null, set `ctor` to
 undefined.
6. If `ctor` is undefined, return
 ? [ArrayCreate](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-arraycreate)(`length`).
7. If
 [IsConstructor](abstract-operations.html#sec-isconstructor)(`ctor`)
 is false, throw a TypeError exception.
8. Return
 ? [Construct](abstract-operations.html#sec-construct)(`ctor`,
 «
 [𝔽](notational-conventions.html#%F0%9D%94%BD)(`length`) »).

[Note]

::: note-contents
If `originalArray` was created using the standard built-in
Array [constructor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#constructor)
for a [realm](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm) that is
not the [realm](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm) of
the [running execution
context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context),
then a new Array is created using the
[realm](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#realm) of the
[running execution
context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#running-execution-context).
This maintains compatibility with Web browsers that have historically
had that behaviour for the `Array.prototype`
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) that now are
defined using ArraySpeciesCreate.

# [10.4.2.4] ArraySetLength ( `array`, `propertyDesc` )

The abstract operation ArraySetLength takes arguments `array`
(an Array) and `propertyDesc` (a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. If `propertyDesc` does not have a `[[Value]]` field, then
 1. Return
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", `propertyDesc`).
2. Let `newLengthDesc` be a copy of
 `propertyDesc`.
3. [Let `newLength` be
 ? [ToUint32](abstract-operations.html#sec-touint32)(`propertyDesc`.`[[Value]]`).]
4. [Let `numberLength` be
 ? [ToNumber](abstract-operations.html#sec-tonumber)(`propertyDesc`.`[[Value]]`).]
5. If
 [SameValueZero](abstract-operations.html#sec-samevaluezero)(`newLength`,
 `numberLength`) is false, throw a RangeError exception.
6. Set `newLengthDesc`.`[[Value]]` to
 `newLength`.
7. Let `oldLengthDesc` be
 [OrdinaryGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarygetownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\").
8. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `oldLengthDesc` is not undefined.
9. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 [IsDataDescriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-isdatadescriptor)(`oldLengthDesc`)
 is true.
10. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `oldLengthDesc`.`[[Configurable]]` is
 false.
11. Let `oldLength` be
 `oldLengthDesc`.`[[Value]]`.
12. If `newLength` ≥ `oldLength`, then
 1. Return
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", `newLengthDesc`).
13. If `oldLengthDesc`.`[[Writable]]` is
 false, return false.
14. If `newLengthDesc` does not have a
 `[[Writable]]` field or
 `newLengthDesc`.`[[Writable]]` is true,
 then
 1. Let `newWritable` be true.
15. Else,
 1. [NOTE](notational-conventions.html#note-step): Setting the
 `[[Writable]]`
 [attribute](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#attribute) to
 false is deferred in case any elements cannot be deleted.
 2. Let `newWritable` be false.
 3. Set `newLengthDesc`.`[[Writable]]`
 to true.
16. Let `succeeded` be
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", `newLengthDesc`).
17. If `succeeded` is false, return false.
18. For each [own
 property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) key
 `propertyKey` of `array` such that
 `propertyKey` is an [array
 index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index) and
 ! [ToUint32](abstract-operations.html#sec-touint32)(`propertyKey`)
 ≥ `newLength`, in descending numeric index order, do
 1. Let `deleteSucceeded` be
 ! `array`.[\[\[Delete\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-delete)(`propertyKey`).
 2. If `deleteSucceeded` is false, then
 1. Set `newLengthDesc`.`[[Value]]`
 to
 ! [ToUint32](abstract-operations.html#sec-touint32)(`propertyKey`) +
 1~𝔽~.
 2. If `newWritable` is false, set
 `newLengthDesc`.`[[Writable]]`
 to false.
 3. Perform
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", `newLengthDesc`).
 4. Return false.
19. If `newWritable` is false, then
 1. Set `succeeded` to
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`array`,
 \"length\", PropertyDescriptor { `[[Writable]]`: false }).
 2. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `succeeded` is true.
20. Return true.

[Note]

::: note-contents
In steps
[3](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#step-arraysetlength-newlen)
and
[4](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#step-arraysetlength-numberlen),
if `propertyDesc`.`[[Value]]` is an object
then its `valueOf`
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) is called twice.
This is legacy behaviour that was specified with this effect starting
with the 2^nd^ Edition of this specification.

# [10.4.3] String Exotic Objects

A [String
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
is an [exotic
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object) that
encapsulates a String value and exposes virtual
[integer-indexed](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#integer-index)
[data properties](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type)
corresponding to the individual code unit elements of the String value.
[String exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
always have a [data
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type) named
\"length\" whose value is the length of the encapsulated String value.
Both the code unit [data
properties](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type) and
the \"length\" property are non-writable and non-configurable.

An object is a [String exotic
object] (or simply, a [String
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object))
if its `[[GetOwnProperty]]`,
`[[DefineOwnProperty]]`, and
`[[OwnPropertyKeys]]` internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
following implementations, and its other essential internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
definitions found in
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots).
These [methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) are
installed in
[StringCreate](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-stringcreate).

[String exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
have the same internal slots as [ordinary
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object). They
also have a `[[StringData]]` internal slot.

# [10.4.3.1] `[[GetOwnProperty]]` ( `propertyKey` )

The `[[GetOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [String
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
`string` takes argument `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and returns a
[normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
either a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type)
or undefined. It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `propertyDesc` be
 [OrdinaryGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarygetownproperty)(`string`,
 `propertyKey`).
2. If `propertyDesc` is not undefined, return
 `propertyDesc`.
3. Return
 [StringGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-stringgetownproperty)(`string`,
 `propertyKey`).

# [10.4.3.2] `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` ( `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc` )

The `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [String
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
`string` takes arguments `propertyKey` (a
[property key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and
`propertyDesc` (a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type))
and returns a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean. It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `stringDesc` be
 [StringGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-stringgetownproperty)(`string`,
 `propertyKey`).
2. If `stringDesc` is not undefined, then
 1. Let `extensible` be
 `string`.`[[Extensible]]`.
 2. Return
 [IsCompatiblePropertyDescriptor](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-iscompatiblepropertydescriptor)(`extensible`,
 `propertyDesc`, `stringDesc`).
3. Return
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`string`,
 `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc`).

# [10.4.3.3] `[[OwnPropertyKeys]]` ( )

The `[[OwnPropertyKeys]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of a [String
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object)
`obj` takes no arguments and returns a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a
[List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)
of [property keys](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `keys` be a new empty
 [List](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-list-and-record-specification-type).
2. Let `string` be
 `obj`.`[[StringData]]`.
3. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): `string`
 [is a
 String](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type).
4. Let `length` be the length of `string`.
5. For each [integer](notational-conventions.html#integer)
 `i` such that 0 ≤ `i` \< `length`,
 in ascending order, do
 1. Append
 ! [ToString](abstract-operations.html#sec-tostring)([𝔽](notational-conventions.html#%F0%9D%94%BD)(`i`))
 to `keys`.
6. For each [own
 property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) key
 `propertyKey` of `obj` such that
 `propertyKey` is an [array
 index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index) and
 ! [ToIntegerOrInfinity](abstract-operations.html#sec-tointegerorinfinity)(`propertyKey`)
 ≥ `length`, in ascending numeric index order, do
 1. Append `propertyKey` to `keys`.
7. For each [own
 property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) key
 `propertyKey` of `obj` such that
 `propertyKey` [is a
 String](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type)
 and `propertyKey` is not an [array
 index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index), in
 ascending chronological order of property creation, do
 1. Append `propertyKey` to `keys`.
8. For each [own
 property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#own-property) key
 `propertyKey` of `obj` such that
 `propertyKey` [is a
 Symbol](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types-symbol-type),
 in ascending chronological order of property creation, do
 1. Append `propertyKey` to `keys`.
9. Return `keys`.

# [10.4.3.4] StringCreate ( `value`, `proto` )

The abstract operation StringCreate takes arguments `value`
(a String) and `proto` (an Object) and returns a [String
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object).
It is used to specify the creation of new [String exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `string` be
 [MakeBasicObject](abstract-operations.html#sec-makebasicobject)(«
 `[[Prototype]]`, `[[Extensible]]`, `[[StringData]]` »).
2. Set `string`.`[[Prototype]]` to
 `proto`.
3. Set `string`.`[[StringData]]` to
 `value`.
4. Set
 `string`.[\[\[GetOwnProperty\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-getownproperty)
 as specified in
 [10.4.3.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-string-exotic-objects-getownproperty-p).
5. Set
 `string`.[\[\[DefineOwnProperty\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-defineownproperty)
 as specified in
 [10.4.3.2](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-string-exotic-objects-defineownproperty-p-desc).
6. Set
 `string`.[\[\[OwnPropertyKeys\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-ownpropertykeys)
 as specified in
 [10.4.3.3](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-string-exotic-objects-ownpropertykeys).
7. Let `length` be the length of `value`.
8. Perform
 ! [DefinePropertyOrThrow](abstract-operations.html#sec-definepropertyorthrow)(`string`,
 \"length\", PropertyDescriptor { `[[Value]]`:
 [𝔽](notational-conventions.html#%F0%9D%94%BD)(`length`),
 `[[Writable]]`: false, `[[Enumerable]]`: false, `[[Configurable]]`: false }).
9. Return `string`.

# [10.4.3.5] StringGetOwnProperty ( `string`, `propertyKey` )

The abstract operation StringGetOwnProperty takes arguments
`string` (a [String exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#string-exotic-object))
and `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and returns a
[Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type)
or undefined. It performs the following steps when called:

1. If `propertyKey` [is not a
 String](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type),
 return undefined.
2. Let `numericIndex` be
 [CanonicalNumericIndexString](abstract-operations.html#sec-canonicalnumericindexstring)(`propertyKey`).
3. If `numericIndex` is not an [integral
 Number](notational-conventions.html#integral-number), return
 undefined.
4. If `numericIndex` is -0~𝔽~ or `numericIndex`
 \< -0~𝔽~, return undefined.
5. Let `stringData` be
 `string`.`[[StringData]]`.
6. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert):
 `stringData` [is a
 String](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type).
7. Let `length` be the length of `stringData`.
8. If
 [ℝ](notational-conventions.html#%E2%84%9D)(`numericIndex`)
 ≥ `length`, return undefined.
9. Let `resultString` be the
 [substring](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#substring) of
 `stringData` from
 [ℝ](notational-conventions.html#%E2%84%9D)(`numericIndex`)
 to
 [ℝ](notational-conventions.html#%E2%84%9D)(`numericIndex`) +
 1.
10. Return the PropertyDescriptor { `[[Value]]`:
 `resultString`, `[[Writable]]`: false,
 `[[Enumerable]]`: true,
 `[[Configurable]]`: false }.

# [10.4.4] Arguments Exotic Objects

Most ECMAScript
[functions](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) make
an arguments object available to their code. Depending upon the
characteristics of the
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
definition, its arguments object is either an [ordinary
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object) or an
[arguments exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object).
An [arguments exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
is an [exotic
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#exotic-object) whose
[array index](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#array-index)
properties map to the formal parameters bindings of an invocation of its
associated ECMAScript
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object).

An object is an [arguments exotic object] if its internal
[methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) use the
following implementations, with the ones not specified here using those
found in
[10.1](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots).
These [methods](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) are
installed in
[CreateMappedArgumentsObject](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-createmappedargumentsobject).

[Note 1]

::: note-contents
While
[CreateUnmappedArgumentsObject](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-createunmappedargumentsobject)
is grouped into this clause, it creates an [ordinary
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object), not an
[arguments exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object).

[Arguments exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
have the same internal slots as [ordinary
objects](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object). They
also have a `[[ParameterMap]]` internal slot. Ordinary
arguments objects also have a `[[ParameterMap]]`
internal slot whose value is always undefined. For ordinary argument
objects the `[[ParameterMap]]` internal slot is only
used by `Object.prototype.toString`
([20.1.3.6](fundamental-objects.html#sec-object.prototype.tostring)) to
identify them as such.

[Note 2]

::: note-contents
The
[integer-indexed](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#integer-index)
[data properties](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type)
of an [arguments exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
whose numeric name values are less than the number of formal parameters
of the corresponding [function
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object) initially
share their values with the corresponding argument bindings in the
[function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)\'s
[execution
context](executable-code-and-execution-contexts.html#sec-execution-contexts).
This means that changing the property changes the corresponding value of
the argument binding and vice-versa. This correspondence is broken if
such a property is deleted and then redefined or if the property is
changed into an [accessor
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type). If the
arguments object is an [ordinary
object](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#ordinary-object), the
values of its properties are simply a copy of the arguments passed to
the [function](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#function-object)
and there is no dynamic linkage between the property values and the
formal parameter values.

[Note 3]

::: note-contents
The ParameterMap object and its property values are used as a device for
specifying the arguments object correspondence to argument bindings. The
ParameterMap object and the objects that are the values of its
properties are not directly observable from ECMAScript code. An
ECMAScript implementation does not need to actually create or use such
objects to implement the specified semantics.

[Note 4]

::: note-contents
Ordinary arguments objects define a non-configurable [accessor
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type) named
\"callee\" which throws a TypeError exception on access. The \"callee\"
property has a more specific meaning for [arguments exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object),
which are created only for some class of [non-strict
functions](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#non-strict-function).
The definition of this property in the ordinary variant exists to ensure
that it is not defined in any other manner by conforming ECMAScript
implementations.

[Note 5]

::: note-contents
ECMAScript implementations of [arguments exotic
objects](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
have historically contained an [accessor
property](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-object-type) named
\"caller\". Prior to ECMAScript 2017, this specification included the
definition of a throwing \"caller\" property on ordinary arguments
objects. Since implementations do not contain this extension any longer,
ECMAScript 2017 dropped the requirement for a throwing \"caller\"
accessor.

# [10.4.4.1] `[[GetOwnProperty]]` ( `propertyKey` )

The `[[GetOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [arguments
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
`args` takes argument `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and returns a
[normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
either a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type)
or undefined. It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `propertyDesc` be
 [OrdinaryGetOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarygetownproperty)(`args`,
 `propertyKey`).
2. If `propertyDesc` is undefined, return undefined.
3. Let `map` be
 `args`.`[[ParameterMap]]`.
4. Let `isMapped` be
 ! [HasOwnProperty](abstract-operations.html#sec-hasownproperty)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
5. If `isMapped` is true, then
 1. Set `propertyDesc`.`[[Value]]` to
 ! [Get](abstract-operations.html#sec-get-o-p)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
6. Return `propertyDesc`.

# [10.4.4.2] `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` ( `propertyKey`, `propertyDesc` )

The `[[DefineOwnProperty]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [arguments
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
`args` takes arguments `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and
`propertyDesc` (a [Property
Descriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type))
and returns a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean. It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `map` be
 `args`.`[[ParameterMap]]`.
2. Let `isMapped` be
 ! [HasOwnProperty](abstract-operations.html#sec-hasownproperty)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
3. Let `newArgDesc` be `propertyDesc`.
4. If `isMapped` is true and
 [IsDataDescriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-isdatadescriptor)(`propertyDesc`)
 is true, then
 1. If `propertyDesc` does not have a
 `[[Value]]` field, `propertyDesc`
 has a `[[Writable]]` field, and
 `propertyDesc`.`[[Writable]]` is
 false, then
 1. Set `newArgDesc` to a copy of
 `propertyDesc`.
 2. Set `newArgDesc`.`[[Value]]` to
 ! [Get](abstract-operations.html#sec-get-o-p)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
5. Let `allowed` be
 ! [OrdinaryDefineOwnProperty](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinarydefineownproperty)(`args`,
 `propertyKey`, `newArgDesc`).
6. If `allowed` is false, return false.
7. If `isMapped` is true, then
 1. If
 [IsAccessorDescriptor](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-isaccessordescriptor)(`propertyDesc`)
 is true, then
 1. Perform
 ! `map`.[\[\[Delete\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-delete)(`propertyKey`).
 2. Else,
 1. If `propertyDesc` has a `[[Value]]` field, then
 1. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): The
 following Set will succeed, since formal parameters
 mapped by arguments objects are always writable.
 2. Perform
 ! [Set](abstract-operations.html#sec-set-o-p-v-throw)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`,
 `propertyDesc`.`[[Value]]`,
 false).
 2. If `propertyDesc` has a `[[Writable]]` field and
 `propertyDesc`.`[[Writable]]`
 is false, then
 1. Perform
 ! `map`.[\[\[Delete\]\]](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#internal-method-delete)(`propertyKey`).
8. Return true.

# [10.4.4.3] `[[Get]]` ( `propertyKey`, `receiver` )

The `[[Get]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [arguments
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
`args` takes arguments `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and
`receiver` (an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)
or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. Let `map` be
 `args`.`[[ParameterMap]]`.
2. Let `isMapped` be
 ! [HasOwnProperty](abstract-operations.html#sec-hasownproperty)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
3. If `isMapped` is false, return
 ? [OrdinaryGet](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinaryget)(`args`,
 `propertyKey`, `receiver`).
4. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): `map`
 contains a formal parameter mapping for `propertyKey`.
5. Return
 ! [Get](abstract-operations.html#sec-get-o-p)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).

# [10.4.4.4] `[[Set]]` ( `propertyKey`, `value`, `receiver` )

The `[[Set]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [arguments
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
`args` takes arguments `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)),
`value` (an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types)),
and `receiver` (an [ECMAScript language
value](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-ecmascript-language-types))
and returns either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
It performs the following steps when called:

1. If
 [SameValue](abstract-operations.html#sec-samevalue)(`args`,
 `receiver`) is false, then
 1. Let `isMapped` be false.
2. Else,
 1. Let `map` be
 `args`.`[[ParameterMap]]`.
 2. Let `isMapped` be
 ! [HasOwnProperty](abstract-operations.html#sec-hasownproperty)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`).
3. If `isMapped` is true, then
 1. [Assert](notational-conventions.html#assert): The following Set
 will succeed, since formal parameters mapped by arguments
 objects are always writable.
 2. Perform
 ! [Set](abstract-operations.html#sec-set-o-p-v-throw)(`map`,
 `propertyKey`, `value`, false).
4. Return
 ? [OrdinarySet](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-ordinaryset)(`args`,
 `propertyKey`, `value`,
 `receiver`).

# [10.4.4.5] `[[Delete]]` ( `propertyKey` )

The `[[Delete]]` internal
[method](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#method) of an [arguments
exotic
object](ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#arguments-exotic-object)
`args` takes argument `propertyKey` (a [property
key](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#property-key)) and returns
either a [normal completion
containing](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type)
a Boolean or a [throw
completion](ecmascript-data-types-and-values.html#sec-completion-record-specification-type).
